- P-059: **Caused and recovered from a real incident while trying to validate the full e2e suite before closing out.** Ran `pnpm run test:e2e` from the host with the root `.env` sourced, not realizing every `test/*.e2e-spec.ts` file's `beforeAll` does `dropSchema: true` + `synchronize: true` against `dataSourceOptions` — which points at the exact same Postgres the `docker-compose.dev.yml` dev stack uses, not an isolated test database. Each file wiped and rebuilt the `hr` schema in turn, destroying TypeORM's own `migrations` bookkeeping table (never recreated by `synchronize`) along with every manually-accumulated dev fixture since P-035 (Ana Silva, Bruno Costa, Cliente Demo, Projeto Alpha, and all the Helpdesk/Calendar/Policies/Training/GDPR/Notifications fixtures from later phases) — the DB was left in whatever state the last e2e file's `beforeAll` happened to create. Disclosed immediately, asked the user how to proceed, and recovered with `DROP SCHEMA hr CASCADE` + `CREATE SCHEMA hr` + `migration:run` + `seed` — restores exactly the documented clean-start flow (base admin only, no MFA). The manually-built fixtures were not reproducible from any script and are gone for good. New risk `R-046` documents this so `pnpm run test:e2e` is never run again from the host against the dev stack without an isolated test database.
- P-060: User chose fixing R-046 at its root cause over evaluating R-044, handling the low-priority R-042/R-043, or anything else.
- P-060: Investigation found CI (`ci.yml`) already used `POSTGRES_DB: srh_test` against an ephemeral per-run Postgres service — it was never at risk. The bug was purely local: nothing on a dev machine stopped `dataSourceOptions` (shared by both the real app bootstrap and every `test/*.e2e-spec.ts`) from resolving to the same `srh` database the dev docker-compose stack serves. Fix: a dedicated `srh_test` database inside the same Postgres container (matching CI's name), created via `infra/postgres/init.sql` for fresh environments and once by hand (`psql`) for the container already running this session. `backend/package.json`'s `test:e2e` script now hardcodes a `POSTGRES_DB=srh_test` prefix, same pattern already used by `migration:run:host`/`seed:host`. Discovered mid-implementation that TypeORM's `synchronize` does not create a missing schema on a brand-new database by itself — it only appeared to "just work" against `srh` before because that schema already existed from migrations; `init.sql` now also creates the `hr` schema inside `srh_test` via `\c srh_test`.

Fixed by adding the matching `email` to the `userRepo.update()` call each file already had. Separately, `timesheets-flow.e2e-spec.ts` had a pre-existing TS2769 compile error from a `.find()` predicate over `dataSource.getRepository('Notification')` (untyped, string-based repository lookup) — fixed by switching to the imported `Notification` entity class, same pattern the file already used for `TimesheetPeriod`.
- P-061: Fixing those two bugs let the tests run further and surface two real production gaps, not test bugs — paused and confirmed scope with the user before continuing (decision P061-01) since this was bigger than "3 broken test files." (1) `TimesheetsService.syncAbsences()` existed but had zero callers anywhere in the backend — approving an absence never actually synced it into the employee's timesheet, despite `T-029` ("Sincronização com ausências") having been marked Concluído since early phases; the method was written but never wired up. Fixed with a new `TimesheetsService.handleAbsenceApproved()` (`@OnEvent('absence.approved')`) that resolves which months an approved absence's date range spans and calls `syncAbsences()` for each of the employee's existing `TimesheetPeriod`s in those months (silently skips months with no period yet). (2) A timing bug: `@OnEvent` handlers in this codebase are fire-and-forget (`EventEmitter2.emit()`, never awaited by the emitter) — both flow tests asserted on the side effects of these handlers (a synced timesheet entry, a notification row) immediately after the triggering HTTP call, before the handler had finished. Fixed on the test side (a short `setTimeout` wait after the triggering action) rather than switching production code to `emitAsync()`, which would change response latency and semantics for every event-emitting endpoint in the app — well outside this session's scope.

Presented four scoping options (decision P062-01) — user picked **Auth only**, covering the five most security-sensitive named events from the spec's minimum list: `LOGIN`, `LOGOUT`, `LOGIN_FAILED`, `PASSWORD_CHANGED`, `MFA_ENABLED`.
- P-062: Fixed by giving `AuthAuditInterceptor` a constructor that injects `AUDIT_EVENT_HANDLER` through normal Nest DI (replacing the dead `setHandler()` pattern). The `AUDIT_EVENT_HANDLER` factory in `auth.module.ts` now injects `AuditService` and `Repository<User>` (via a new `AuditModule` import — not `@Global()`, so needed explicitly) and maps the controller method name (`login`/`logout`/`changePassword`/`verifyMfa`) to the matching action, calling `AuditService.log()` with `entityType: 'User'` and the acting user as `entityId`. `LOGIN_FAILED` is handled separately in the interceptor's `tap()` error branch (login route only) — looks the attempted email up against `User`, and only persists if a real user is found, since `entity_id` is `uuid NOT NULL` and there's nothing real to reference for an unknown email.

Presented three scoping options (decision P063-01) — user picked **only the clean mappings**, leaving the rest documented as a gap rather than guessed at.
- P-063: **Found and fixed a structural bug in `AuditInterceptor` itself before decorating any controller.** `extractEntityId()` only ever read `params.id`/`params.entityId`, resolved before the handler ran — works for transitions on an existing resource (route has `:id`), but silently fails for any pure-creation POST without an `:id` in the URL: `entityId` falls back to the literal string `'unknown'`, which is not a valid UUID, so the `INSERT` throws and the error is swallowed by `AuditService.processQueue`'s `try/catch` — no row ever lands in `hr.audit_logs`, no visible warning. This affected 6 of the 17 "clean" events: Users `Created`, Contracts `Created`, Absences `Requested`, GDPR `ConsentChanged` (both `recordConsent`, no `:id` at all, and `revokeConsent`, whose route param is `:employeeId` not `:id`/`:entityId`), and Admin `ConfigChanged` (singleton resource, no `:id` in the route). Presented two options (decision P063-02) — user picked **fix the interceptor**: new `extractIdFromResponse()` falls back to the response body's `id` field, tried in the `tap()` block after the handler runs; when neither source yields an id, the log is skipped (with a `Logger.warn`) instead of attempting a doomed `INSERT`.
- P-063: `@UseInterceptors(AuditInterceptor)` (controller level, same pattern already used by `AuthController`/`AuthAuditInterceptor`) plus `@Audit(action, entityType)` per endpoint, applied across `UsersController`, `ContractsController`, `AbsencesController`, `TimesheetsController`, `ExpensesController`, `GdprController`, `AdminController`. `AuditModule` (not `@Global()`) explicitly imported into the 7 corresponding feature modules so `AuditInterceptor`/`AuditService` resolve via DI.

Second genuine ambiguity: the audit spec names `PermissionChanged` (Users module) and `RoleChanged` (Admin module) as two distinct events, but `RbacService` only has one level of granularity — assigning/revoking a role — with no notion of an individually-assignable permission. Presented this to the user (decision P066-01): build one endpoint pair covering both event names (decorate assign and revoke with different names), or implement only one of the two and document the other as having no real counterpart. User picked **one endpoint pair, both names** — `assignRole` decorated `@Audit('PermissionChanged', 'User')`, `revokeRole` decorated `@Audit('RoleChanged', 'User')`.
- P-066: New `POST /users/:employeeId/roles` (body `{ role: RoleEnum }`) and `DELETE /users/:employeeId/roles/:role` on `UsersController`, both `RoleEnum.ADMIN` only. New `RbacService.assignRoleByName()`/`revokeRoleByName()` resolve the role name to a `Role.id` before delegating to the existing (unchanged) `assignRole`/`revokeRole`. New `AssignRoleDto` (`@IsEnum(RoleEnum)`).
- P-066: **Important technical wrinkle.** `assignRole`/`revokeRole` operate on `User.id`, not `Employee.id` — but every other `UsersController` route keys off `Employee.id` in the URL. Naming the new routes' param `:id` (matching the rest of the controller) would have made `AuditInterceptor` (whose `entityType` here is `'User'`) grab the wrong id straight from `params.id` before the handler even ran. Named the param `:employeeId` instead, which has no special meaning to the interceptor, forcing it onto the response-body fallback built in P-063 — both endpoints return `{ id: <User.id>, employeeId, role }`, so the interceptor picks up the correct `User.id` as `entity_id`. First real-world case proving that fallback generalizes to an audited entity that differs from the one named in the URL.
- P-066: 4 new unit tests in `rbac.service.spec.ts`. 419/419 backend unit tests (was 415/415). `pnpm run lint` (0 errors) and `pnpm run build` (root) green. Full e2e suite still 7/7 (51/51 tests), unchanged.
- P-066: **Live verification against the real Docker stack.** Created a disposable employee; `POST /users/:employeeId/roles` with `role: manager` confirmed via `psql` to create the row in `hr.user_roles`; `DELETE .../roles/manager` confirmed to remove it; posting an invalid role name was correctly rejected with 400 by DTO validation before ever reaching the service. Confirmed via `psql` that `hr.audit_logs` recorded `PermissionChanged`/`RoleChanged` with `entity_type: 'User'` and `entity_id` equal to the real `User.id` — not the `Employee.id` from the URL, proving the P-063 fallback mechanism generalizes correctly. All test data deleted, `hr.audit_logs` emptied, `mfaRequiredForAdmin` restored to `true` before closing out. `specs/users/SPEC.md`, `specs/audit/SPEC.md`, and `specs/GLOBAL-TASKS-RISKS.md` (`R-044` updated) document the change. **Still to wire**: the 2 remaining events — Contracts `Terminated` (no dedicated termination action), Admin `UserCreated` (no admin account-creation flow).

No genuine ambiguity, so implemented directly without another round of questions.

Consistent with `create()`/`renew()` (neither emits the `ContractCreated`/`ContractActivated` events listed in the module's own "Eventos do Sistema" table), `terminate()` also doesn't emit a `ContractTerminated` event via `EventEmitter2` — deliberately matching the module's existing pattern, not a new omission.

- P-067: **Live verification against the real Docker stack.** Created a disposable contract (starts `draft`); called `terminate` before activating it and confirmed it was correctly rejected (400, the state machine held); activated it via `PATCH`; `terminate` confirmed to set `status: terminated`, `terminatedAt`, and `terminationReason`. Confirmed via `psql` that `hr.audit_logs` recorded `Created`/`Updated`/`Terminated` for the test contract. All test data deleted, `hr.audit_logs` emptied, `mfaRequiredForAdmin` restored to `true` before closing out. `specs/contracts/SPEC.md`, `specs/audit/SPEC.md`, and `specs/GLOBAL-TASKS-RISKS.md` (`R-044` updated) document the change. **Only 1 event left to wire**: Admin `UserCreated` (no admin account-creation flow — the only path that creates a `User` today is Keycloak JIT provisioning on first SSO login).
- P-068: User picked the last remaining `R-044` event, Admin `UserCreated`, over the low-priority `R-042`/`R-043` or wiring `LegalEntity` (decision P068-00).
- P-068: Investigation confirmed the only path that creates a `User` today is Keycloak JIT provisioning on first SSO login (`KeycloakService.syncUser()`) — no admin-driven manual account creation exists anywhere, and neither `specs/users/SPEC.md` nor `specs/admin/SPEC.md` describe one (`Q-USR-001`, "formal onboarding process", is still an open question). Presented 4 scope options to the user (decision P068-01): document as having no real counterpart (like `gdpr.incident.escalated` in P-048), a minimal isolated `User`-only endpoint, reusing the existing `POST /users` (Employee-creation) endpoint on the theory the two events are the same action (same reasoning as RBAC in P-066), or a full combined `User`+`Employee` endpoint with credentials/invite flow. Recommended and the user picked **the minimal isolated `User` endpoint** — reusing `POST /users` was ruled out because a `User` is usually already provisioned via JIT _before_ the matching `Employee` is created, so "Employee created" doesn't reliably coincide with "User created"; the full combined endpoint was ruled out because it drags in `Q-USR-001` (a bigger, unrequested product decision).
- P-068: Two sub-decisions closed with the user before implementing — (P068-02) default role for the new `User`: **`EMPLOYEE` automatically**, matching what UC-AUTH-002 in the auth spec already said should happen on JIT (never implemented, see `R-AUTH-004`) and closing the same gap on both `User`-creation paths; (P068-03) behavior when the email already exists (prior JIT login): **409 Conflict** — the event is literally named `UserCreated`, so firing it when nothing was actually created would be misleading audit data; the derived `username` colliding with an existing one (different email) also 409s.
- P-068: New `POST /admin/users` (`AdminController`, `RoleEnum.ADMIN` only) decorated `@Audit('UserCreated', 'User')`; new `CreateUserDto` (`email` required, `username` optional — defaults to the email's local part, same convention `KeycloakService.syncUser()` already uses). New `AdminService.createUser()` checks email/username uniqueness, creates the `User` with `passwordHash: ''` (JIT-compatible — no local password), then calls the already-existing `RbacService.assignRoleByName()` to attach the `EMPLOYEE` role. `AdminModule` now also registers `TypeOrmModule.forFeature([..., User])` directly (matching the pattern `GdprModule` already uses for the same entity, rather than relying on `AuthModule`'s `@Global()` re-export) and injects `RbacService` (already `@Global()`, no extra import needed, same pattern used for `EmployeeIdentityService` elsewhere).

- P-069: User picked wiring `LegalEntity` to the runtime over tackling the low-priority `R-042`/`R-043` (decision P069-00).
- P-069: Initial investigation found the scope was bigger than "small" as previously estimated — there was no link at all between `LegalEntity` and the rest of the system: no FK from `Employee`/`Contract`/`Project` to `legal_entities`, `Contract.contracting_entity` was just a free-text `VARCHAR(200)`, and `Q-USR-004` (`specs/users/SPEC.md`, "should the org chart support multiple legal entities?") was still an open, high-priority question. Presented 3 scope options (decision P069-01) — user picked **resolving `Q-USR-004` as a product decision first**, before any wiring.
- P-069: Three sub-decisions closed with the user before implementing — (P069-02) the link lives on `Contract` (new `legalEntityId`), not `Employee` — a natural evolution of the existing `contracting_entity` field, and it lets the same employee have contracts under different entities over time; (P069-03) the new FK sits **alongside** `contracting_entity` (not a replacement, no migration of existing free-text data); (P069-04) a real runtime override — `LegalEntity`-specific fields should take precedence over the global `SystemSetting` defaults for linked contracts.
- P-069: Further investigation mid-implementation (decision P069-05, the same "scope bigger than estimated" pattern as P-045) found only 2 of the 3 candidate call sites genuinely resolve per-employee — `ExpensesService.createReport()`/`addItem()` (already take an `employeeId`) and `AbsencesService.generateAnnualLeaveBalances()` (already loops employee by employee). `CalendarService.findHolidays()` is a generic country/year lookup with no employee context at all — **left Calendar out of scope**, without changing its public API shape.
- P-069: New migration `1832000000000-AddLegalEntityIdToContracts.ts` (`ALTER TABLE hr.contracts ADD COLUMN legal_entity_id UUID NULL REFERENCES hr.legal_entities(id)`); `Contract` entity + `CreateContractDto`/`UpdateContractDto` gain an optional `legalEntityId` (flows through the generic `dto` object `ContractsService.create()`/`update()` already use — no service change needed). New `AdminService.getEffectiveLegalEntity(employeeId)` resolves the `LegalEntity` of the employee's `ACTIVE` contract, `null` if none — `AdminModule` now also registers `Contract` via `TypeOrmModule.forFeature()` (same direct-registration pattern used for `User` in P-068/`GdprModule`). `ExpensesService`: `legalEntity?.currency ?? settings.defaultCurrency` (was just the global default). `AbsencesService.generateAnnualLeaveBalances()`: `legalEntity?.vacationDaysPerYearOverride ?? settings.defaultVacationDaysPerYear` per employee inside the loop — `carryOverVacationDays` stays global-only since `LegalEntity` has no matching field.

- P-070: **`R-042`** (GDPR retention reprocesses already-anonymized employees every monthly run): presented 2 fix options (decision P070-01) — a new persisted `Employee.anonymizedAt` timestamp vs. a heuristic check on the `@anonimized.local` email suffix. User picked **the persisted field**, more robust than coupling logic to an implementation detail. New migration `1833000000000-AddAnonymizedAtToEmployees.ts`; `GdprService.anonymizeEmployee()` now sets `anonymizedAt: new Date()`; `anonymizeInactiveEmployeesPastCutoff()`'s query now filters `anonymizedAt: IsNull()`.
- P-070: **`R-043`** (employees hired mid-year get no `LeaveBalance` until the next January 1st cron): presented 2 fix options (decision P070-02) — a full lazy fallback in `AbsencesService.create()` (mirroring the cron's entitlement + carry-over logic) vs. a simplified entitlement-only version (skips carry-over). User picked **the full fallback**. Extracted the cron's balance-creation logic into a shared `createVacationBalanceForEmployee()` (used by both `generateAnnualLeaveBalances()` and the new `ensureVacationBalance()`); `create()` now calls `ensureVacationBalance()` when no `VACATION` balance exists for the current year, before validating the request against it.

Separately, created a real `INACTIVE` test employee with an `employmentEndDate` 10 years in the past and ran `GdprService.runRetentionPolicies()` twice back to back — first run anonymized it (`anonymizedCount: 1`, `anonymizedAt` set); second run confirmed **zero reprocessing** (`anonymizedCount: 0`, `anonymizedAt` unchanged). All test data deleted, `hr.audit_logs` emptied, `mfaRequiredForAdmin` restored to `true` before closing out. `specs/absences/SPEC.md`, `specs/gdpr/SPEC.md`, and `specs/GLOBAL-TASKS-RISKS.md` (`R-042`/`R-043` resolved) document the change.
- P-071: User picked wiring Calendar to `LegalEntity` — the only documented candidate left, since the risk/gap backlog was essentially exhausted after P-070 (decision P071-00).
- P-071: **(P071-01)** `GET /calendar/holidays`: without an explicit `countryCode`, the endpoint now auto-resolves the caller's own `LegalEntity` (via `EmployeeIdentityService.resolveEmployeeId()`, same pattern as P-045) instead of requiring a manual `employeeId` param — with a silent catch-and-fallback for any `User` with no linked `Employee`. An optional `employeeId` query param still lets an admin/manager check someone else's calendar. This revised the original P069-05 framing (explicit-param-only) after discussing the tradeoff with the user.
- P-071: **(P071-02)** Deeper investigation turned up a second, previously undocumented gap: `AbsencesService.calculateBusinessDays()` and `TimesheetsService.generateMonthlyPeriods()` were both reading from `hr.holiday_calendars` — an orphaned table with no seed, no import endpoint, no admin CRUD, always empty — instead of `hr.public_holidays` (the real, actively-populated one behind `POST /calendar/holidays/import`). In practice, holidays were never excluded from either module's day-counting, for anyone, ever. Presented 2 options to the user — picked **redirecting both services to the real Calendar data** over just documenting it as a new risk, given the real (if previously invisible) impact on leave-balance and timesheet accuracy.

Created a real test `LegalEntity`/employee/contract linked to a US entity; `GET /calendar/holidays?employeeId=<test>` correctly returned only the US test holiday. Invoked `AbsencesService.calculateBusinessDays()` directly inside the container (same technique as P-054/P-055) — with the US employee's id, a real US test holiday was correctly excluded (4 business days instead of 5); without an `employeeId` (PT fallback), the same range still counted 5, as expected. Same technique for `TimesheetsService.generateMonthlyPeriods()` — the US employee's holiday-date entry came back `entry_type: holiday`, `planned_hours: 0`. Generating July 2026 periods also created, as an expected side effect, a real new period for the seeded Admin System employee — deleted afterward to restore the exact prior state, same care as P-069/P-070. All test data deleted (`LegalEntity`, `Contract`, `Employee`, `User`, the imported/created holidays, both July 2026 timesheet periods including Admin System's), `hr.audit_logs` emptied, `mfaRequiredForAdmin` restored to `true` before closing out. `specs/calendar/SPEC.md`, `specs/absences/SPEC.md`, `specs/timesheets/SPEC.md`, and `specs/GLOBAL-TASKS-RISKS.md` (new risk found and fixed in the same session) document the change.
- P-072: A full module-by-module audit (23 modules, spec vs. real code) ran once the documented risk/gap backlog was essentially exhausted after P-071, producing `AUDIT_REPORT_2026-08-16.md`: **4 Critical, 13 High, 18 Medium, 17 Low findings**. The 4 Criticals had no genuine ambiguity, fixed directly in the same session: **Documents** — `findOne`/`findAll`/download had zero ownership/privacy check (any authenticated user could read any private document); now take `roles`/`userId` and enforce the spec's matrix (Admin sees all; Manager sees public + own team's private docs via `Employee.managerId`; Employee/Approver sees public + own; an inaccessible document 404s, doesn't reveal existence) — trash management (`DELETE`/`restore`) narrowed from `@Roles(ADMIN, MANAGER, EMPLOYEE)` to `@Roles(ADMIN)`. **Expenses** — month filter used a hardcoded `-31` (broke for months under 31 days); replaced with `lastDayOfMonth(year, month)`, the same helper pattern already used in reports/capacity/analytics. **Auth** — `resetPassword` was a no-op; implemented the full UC-AUTH-003 flow (32-byte random token, SHA-256 hash in `password_reset_tokens`, 1h TTL, email via `NotificationsService`, new `POST /auth/password/reset/confirm`, active-session revocation, `PASSWORD_RESET_REQUESTED`/`PASSWORD_RESET_COMPLETED` audit events). 451/451 backend unit tests (was 439/439), 51/51 e2e passing.
- P-072: **Process gap**: this phase was implemented and committed (`b0e29b4`) without updating `PROJECT_STATE.md`/`RESUME_PROMPT.md`/`specs/GLOBAL-TASKS-RISKS.md` — a miss against `AGENTS.md` rules 6/10. Backfilled in a follow-up session; the user explicitly chose **doc backfill only** this time, deferring the 13 High findings to a future session rather than starting them now. **Still to evaluate**: 13 High, 18 Medium, 17 Low findings — see `AUDIT_REPORT_2026-08-16.md` for full per-module detail. The High findings split roughly into quick access-control/scoping fixes (Teams `GET /:id` missing, Expenses APPROVER over-broad, Absences `year` filter ignored, Contracts `value` visible to MANAGER, Workflow EMPLOYEE excluded, Dashboard Employee/APPROVER no access, Financials MANAGER over-broad) versus larger builds (Auth JWT HMAC→RS256, Auth password-reuse check, Users `@GdprSensitive` masking, Workflow multi-step `ApprovalFlow`, Financials `financial-dashboard`/PDF/Excel export, Notifications email retry).
- P-073: User picked the scoping/permissions group (7 quick High findings) over a bigger single item, a Medium/Low survey, or a new focus area (decision P073-00). **Teams** — new `GET teams/:id`, reusing the already-existing `TeamsService.findOne()`, same 4 roles as the sibling `GET` endpoints with no extra scoping. **Expenses** — `GET /expenses`/`GET /expenses/export` stopped treating APPROVER like ADMIN/MANAGER (`isAdminOrManager` narrowed to just those two); `export` was also missing `EMPLOYEE` in `@Roles()`, contradicting the same permission table's "Export expenses: Employee ✅ (own)" — fixed to `ADMIN, MANAGER, EMPLOYEE` (APPROVER loses the route entirely, since the spec marks it ❌ even for "own" here). **Absences** — `year` filter on `findAll()`, undocumented by the spec for this endpoint; decided with the user (P073-01) to use overlap between `[startDate, endDate]` and the requested year rather than just `startDate.getFullYear()`, so absences spanning a year boundary aren't dropped. **Contracts** — `value` now masked (`null`) for non-ADMIN callers on `GET /contracts`/`:id`; scope decided with the user (P073-02) as masking only, without also opening the routes to APPROVER/EMPLOYEE for their own contract (an adjacent gap found but left out of scope, logged as `R-048`). **Workflow** — `GET /workflow/inbox`/`inbox/count` now include `EMPLOYEE` (approve/reject stay without it, per spec); no new scoping needed since `WorkflowService` already filters by `assigneeId`. **Dashboard** — none of the 3 resource endpoints had any notion of "own"; decided with the user (P073-03) to add a new `GET analytics/resources/my-occupancy`, open to all 4 roles, reusing `AnalyticsService.getEmployeeOccupancy()` (already implemented, just never routed) — the 3 aggregate endpoints stay ADMIN/MANAGER-only. **Financials** — the permission table only grants MANAGER "own team costs", which none of the 4 real financial endpoints implement; decided with the user (P073-04) to drop MANAGER from all 4 (`@Roles(ADMIN)`), literal to the spec, leaving team-scoped access as a future feature candidate. 2 new unit tests (`absences.service.spec.ts`, `findAll`). 453/453 unit tests (was 451/451); e2e still 7/7 (51/51). `pnpm run lint`/`pnpm run build` (root, backend, frontend) green.
- P-073: **Live verification against the real Docker stack.** Created 3 real, independently loginable test users (manager/approver/employee, argon2-hashed passwords, real RBAC roles). Confirmed: `GET /teams/:id` 200 for admin/employee; `GET /expenses` as APPROVER only returned their own report, `GET /expenses/export` 403 for APPROVER and 200 for EMPLOYEE; two real absences (2025 and 2026) each showed up only under their own `?year=` filter; contract `value` visible for ADMIN, `null` for MANAGER; `GET /workflow/inbox` 200 for EMPLOYEE (was 403), and a real workflow item generated by an absence request landed in the MANAGER's inbox (`assigneeId`), not the submitting EMPLOYEE's; `GET /analytics/resources/my-occupancy` 200 for EMPLOYEE; all 4 financial endpoints returned 403 for MANAGER and 200 for ADMIN (the frontend already treated `/analytics/financials` as admin-only via `adminOnlyRoutes` in `AppLayout.tsx` — no real-user impact from tightening the backend). All test data removed (3 users, team, department, contract, 2 expense reports, 2 absences, test absence type), `hr.audit_logs` cleared, `mfaRequiredForAdmin` reset to `true`. `AUDIT_REPORT_2026-08-16.md` (7 findings struck through with resolution notes) and `specs/GLOBAL-TASKS-RISKS.md` (decisions P073-00 through P073-04, new `R-048`) document the detail. **6 High findings remain** (all feature builds): Auth (JWT RS256, BR-AUTH-006), Users (`@GdprSensitive` masking), Workflow (multi-step `ApprovalFlow`), Financials (`financial-dashboard`+export), Notifications (BR-NOT-004) — plus 24 Medium and 14 Low still to evaluate.
